**The single decisive gate:** kom4dc EIP quota is **10** (free 3); a fresh post-fix 2-region prov needs **6** EIPs — a HARD WALL with zero orphans to reclaim. Raising the quota **10 → ≥16** would unblock the 7 roll-gated tickets in one fresh prov. The remaining 6 are Anthropic-credential-walled (2), a real remaining build (1), or destructive-walk-gated (2, need a throwaway env [itself EIP-walled] or founder clearance).

The line 3 ms earlier is `handoverjwt: signer ready {"issuer":"https://console.hw292.omani.works"}` — so the Sovereign HAD its own local signer available and still declined to publish it, which is exactly `decidePubkeyPublish` returning `pubkeyPublishPreserveInjected` and returning early before the PATCH. The stored Secret is untouched: single key `public.jwk`, 399 bytes, RSA, sha256[:12]=6ba2fa2e6d3c, age 12h — i.e. the cloud-init-injected value has survived every reconcile since bootstrap rather than being overwritten on any tick. | sha256sum` yields e3b0c44298fc1c14… = sha256("") — a plausible digest from nothing. R18's original pass condition was "the two hashes MATCH", and two ABSENT reads match exactly, so a walker on a Secret-less cluster would have recorded a PASS for a key that is not there. Hardened in `3d98af405`: assert existence, assert the hash is not the empty sentinel, only then compare. Same vacuity class as #5544/#5558 — this time in my own artifact, which is why the spec had to be executed rather than reasoned about. |

(b) BUT the door is only ~50% alive: with the IP pinned (--resolve <host>:443:212.72.24.82) 10 sequential curls gave console.acme-corp 5/10 200 + 5/10 000 ERR_CONNECTION_RESET, console.delta-corp 4/10, console.walk-two 4/10, console.beta-corp 3/6 — while console.hw290.omani.works on the SAME IP is 10/10 200 and a raw TCP connect to 212.72.24.82:443 is 10/10 OK. So it is an SNI-selective TLS reset on the *.<orgslug> wildcard listeners of Gateway kube-system/cilium-gateway-console (listeners console-https-acme-corp=*.acme-corp.omani.homes, -beta-corp, -walk-two, -delta-corp) — the Sovereign's own hostnames on that same gateway are declared as EXACT hostnames and never flap. This is the #5341 multi-gateway-wildcard-SNI family but with a connection-reset symptom rather than envoy-404; it would intermittently break this row's 'loads' clause for a real customer. ‖ — |

| 92 | funnel | [#3376](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3376) | As the signed-in customer, rapidly re-submit checkout/redeem >5× in a few seconds → after ~5 attempts a rate-limit notice appears; a single legitimate redeem is unaffected. | hw292 | ❌ | hw292-2026-08-03: STILL FAILING, and the split is unchanged from the original filing — the SERVER is right and the UI throws its answer away. RATE LIMITER WORKS: a serial burst of 20 POSTs to /api/billing/vouchers/redeem-preview returned 200 200 429 200 200 429 429 429 200 200 429 200 200 429 429 429 429 429 429 429 — 12 of 20 limited, ending in seven CONSECUTIVE 429s, so it clamps and stays clamped under sustained pressure; a parallel burst of 25 gave 8 more 429s. The 429 body is exactly right: {"error":"too many redeem attempts — please wait a few seconds and try again","retry_after":10}. BACKOFF CLAUSE PASSES: after pausing past retry_after, a single request returned 200, and an unpressured serial run of 10 was 200 throughout — a legitimate redeem is genuinely unaffected. WHAT FAILS is the notice, and the cause is now pinned to a specific branch: core/marketplace/src/pages/redeem.astro:163-167 handles 404 and 410 explicitly, then funnels EVERY other non-ok status — 429 included — into `if (!res.ok)`, overwrites the detail with the generic 'Could not validate the voucher right now. Please try again in a moment.' and shows the redeem-not-valid panel. The server's own message and retry_after:10 are discarded, so a rate-limited customer is told the voucher is invalid rather than that they are going too fast. Related limiter-budget issue #5484 is open; this is the UI half. |

Minted as the signed-in owner: POST /api/v1/deployments/1c56518035a83e03/mint-handover-token -> 200 {"redirectURL":"https://console.hw292.omani.works/auth/handover?token=<RS256 JWT>"}, with claims exactly right for a franchised Sovereign — iss and aud both https://console.hw292.omani.works, sub/email emrah.baysal@openova.io, role sovereign-admin, deployment_id 1c56518035a83e03, ttl 300s. Following that URL with a FRESH cookie jar returns 401 {"error":"invalid issuer"}. Three benign explanations ruled out before concluding: NOT expiry (measured at request time iat=1785771497 exp=1785771797 — 250s still to run), NOT client shape (identical 401 with a default curl UA and with a Chrome UA + Accept: text/html), NOT a consumed token (freshly minted, distinct jti). Root cause is a hardcoded literal at products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/auth_handover.go:232 — `const expectedIss = "https://console.openova.io"` — while the MINTER on the same pod resolves handoverjwt.DefaultIssuer() and the pod is configured CATALYST_HANDOVER_JWT_ISSUER=https://console.hw292.omani.works. Minter and verifier disagree on the same process. This is also a Pillar-5 tether: the session gate only trusts tokens issued by the mothership origin. Row re-walkable the moment #5614 lands. |

| 110 | placement | [#3642](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3642) | Gitea opens already signed in (avatar/menu shows the SSO user), repo list renders — no Gitea login form. | hw290 | ⚠️ | hw290-2026-07-27 RE-DIAGNOSED — this is NOT an Open-control defect. The control is CORRECT: launch-url resolves to /user/oauth2/openova-sso and the post-launch DOM carries avatar, 'Sign Out', 'Dashboard' and 'emrah' x11 — it lands genuinely signed in on 3 of 5 fresh-session walks. The 2 misses are the PRE-EXISTING TRANSPORT FLAKE, now quantified: ~14% connection-timeout per request on EVERY hw290 hostname (api 2/12, auth 2/12, newapi 1/12), and the silent-SSO chain is SIX external hops, so 14%/hop compounds to ~55% end-to-end — matching the observed miss rate exactly. One miss surfaced as HTTP 500 rather than a timeout: the back-channel token POST to auth.hw290…/token was reset mid-flight ('read: connection reset by peer' in the gitea pod log) — same fault on the hairpin leg, not a separate bug. NOTE the invalid_client root cause quoted in this row's older evidence is GONE (Keycloak now logs only cookie_not_found on residual misses) — that cause is stale, resolved by #5400/#5403. ‖hw290-2026-07-26: Walked BOTH paths as owner. (a) Console /app/bp-gitea → click data-testid=btn-launch-app → lands on https://auth.hw290.omani.works/realms/sovereign/broker/catalyst-pin/endpoint?code=…&state=…(ru=https://gitea.hw290.omani.works/user/oauth2/openova-sso/callback) → rendered DOM text: 'SOVEREIGN / We are sorry... / Unexpected error when authenticating with identity provider'. (b) Direct https://gitea.hw290.omani.works/ HTTP 502 → same broker error page. Never reaches Gitea; no avatar, no repo list. ROOT CAUSE (first-hand, both sides): keycloak-0 log — `ERROR [org.keycloak.broker.oidc.AbstractOAuth2IdentityProvider] Unexpected response from token endpoint https://api.hw290.omani.works/oidc/token. status=401, response={"error":"invalid_client","error_description":"client auth failed"}` then `type="IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN_ERROR" realmName="sovereign" clientId="gitea" error="identity_provider_login_failure"` (16:05:59Z + 16:07:51Z = my two attempts); catalyst-api log — `POST /oidc/token status=401` on every attempt while `GET /oidc/auth status=302` succeeds. So the catalyst-pin IdP client-secret (Secret catalyst-system/catalyst-pin-broker-credentials key client-secret, env CATALYST_PIN_BROKER_CLIENT_SECRET) does not match what Keycloak's catalyst-pin IdP presents. 12 IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN_ERROR events, ZERO successful IDENTITY_PROVIDER_LOGIN in the whole log window — affects gitea, grafana, newapi-admin, powerdns-admin alike. Earliest occurrence 15:42:51Z (after the 15:19Z keycloak restart from the region-kill drill). Screenshots: pt290-09-bp-gitea-launched.png, pt290-10-gitea.png ‖ —|

| 123 | catalog | [#3668](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/3668) | Handover URL → lands on `/dashboard` already signed-in as the owner (avatar E), no login form (everything below is admin-gated). | hw292 | ⛔ | hw292-2026-08-03: BLOCKED — the signed handover URL cannot be walked because this Sovereign REJECTS ITS OWN handover token, filed as #5614. Minted as the signed-in owner: POST /api/v1/deployments/1c56518035a83e03/mint-handover-token -> 200 {"redirectURL":"https://console.hw292.omani.works/auth/handover?token=<RS256 JWT>"}, with claims exactly right for a franchised Sovereign — iss and aud both https://console.hw292.omani.works, sub/email emrah.baysal@openova.io, role sovereign-admin, deployment_id 1c56518035a83e03, ttl 300s. Following that URL with a FRESH cookie jar returns 401 {"error":"invalid issuer"}. Three benign explanations ruled out before concluding: NOT expiry (measured at request time iat=1785771497 exp=1785771797 — 250s still to run), NOT client shape (identical 401 with a default curl UA and with a Chrome UA + Accept: text/html), NOT a consumed token (freshly minted, distinct jti). Root cause is a hardcoded literal at products/catalyst/bootstrap/api/internal/handler/auth_handover.go:232 — `const expectedIss = "https://console.openova.io"` — while the MINTER on the same pod resolves handoverjwt.DefaultIssuer() and the pod is configured CATALYST_HANDOVER_JWT_ISSUER=https://console.hw292.omani.works. Minter and verifier disagree on the same process. This is also a Pillar-5 tether: the session gate only trusts tokens issued by the mothership origin. Row re-walkable the moment #5614 lands. |

### Governance train — items 187-215 (added 2026-06-21)

These extend the canonical 186-case walk with this session's governance work. **Status is deliberately conservative** (no premature ✅): `⏳` = built/merged in code but **NOT yet validated on the durable live console** (the agents' temporary image rolls were Flux-reverted, so they are not yet what the operator sees — each needs a real end-user walk after the durable roll); `◑` = mechanism proven (e.g. against live HCS) but full in-cluster proof lands on a fresh prov; `❌` = not built yet (fix in flight) or genuinely requires a clean re-prov. A row flips `✅` only after an operator end-user walk on the durable console with pasted evidence, per the TRUST-ledger discipline.

Verified first-hand against the binary actually serving production. BINARY: deploy catalyst/catalyst-api image = ghcr.io/openova-io/openova/catalyst-api:b1b472d. IN THAT EXACT COMMIT: handler/handler.go:177-183 declares `consoleProbe func(fqdn string) error` described as 'the external console-reachability gate applied before ...'; handler/phase1_console_gate_test.go exercises both the pass and the 404-fail shapes. THE DECISIVE CODE — `git show b1b472d:.../handler/phase1_watch.go` lines ~1240-1277 show the OutcomeReady arm setting `dep.Result.ConsoleDegraded = true` + `ConsoleDegradedDetail = consoleReachErr.Error()` and logging 'phase 1 converged on the PRIMARY but the console front door was not confirmed externally reachable — staying ready + firing the producer chain; surfaced as ConsoleDegraded with a background re-probe (#5253, family #4486/#4706)', and then, OUTSIDE and AFTER that if-block, the UNCONDITIONAL `dep.Status = "ready"`. The in-tree comment states the rationale verbatim: latching failed here 'left the ENTIRE cross-region topology permanently inert (hw276...)' and on hw276 the trigger was 'a FALSE NEGATIVE: the console stack was healthy, its SPA root just answered 404 while the front door served fine one redirect later'. So probe failure is surfaced loudly + background-re-probed, but it does NOT gate `ready`. HW290'S OWN RECORD (GET /sovereign/api/v1/deployments/31106b6aa4a7e8e7, HTTP 200): status=ready, phase1Outcome=ready, secondaryDegraded=True, and the consoleDegraded key is ABSENT (omitempty => false) — consistent with console.hw290.omani.works answering 200, i.e. for THIS prov the ready-write did follow a successful external probe. RECOMMENDATION: reconcile the row text with #5253 — 'pre-ready external console probe, with a non-fatal ConsoleDegraded surface + background re-probe' rather than 'gate'. ‖ hw290-2026-07-26: hw290 2026-07-26T16:15Z, mothership read-only (kubectl --context default). The #4706 external console-reachability probe EXISTS and runs in the binary actually serving production: deploy catalyst/catalyst-api image = ghcr.io/openova-io/openova/catalyst-api:b1b472d = commit b1b472dda ('fix(retry): phase-retry must run the post-apply chain… (#5383)') on main. In that exact tree, phase1_watch.go calls `consoleReachErr = h.consoleProbe(dep.Request.SovereignFQDN)` (line 1112) BEFORE the Phase-1 termination write, and defaultConsoleReachable() GETs https://console.<fqdn> browser-shaped (Accept: text/html, redirect-following) requiring a FINAL status < 400 — the <500→<400 tightening that killed the hw218 404 false-green. BUT the row's literal 'writes ready ONLY after an external HTTP answer' is NOT what ships: #5253 deliberately demoted the probe from gate to surface. Verbatim from the running tree (provisioner.go ~1480): 'ConsoleDegraded — surface-only flag (#5253, family #4486/#4706)… Like SecondaryDegraded this is intentionally NOT a gate — markPhase1Done keeps Status="ready" and fires the full producer chain (handover, ClusterMesh establish, spine/adoption/policy hooks) off the converged primary, because latching Status="failed" here left the ENTIRE cross-region topology permanently inert (hw276…)'. And phase1_watch.go:1272 logs 'phase 1 converged on the PRIMARY but the console front door was not confirmed externally reachable — staying ready + firing the producer chain; surfaced as ConsoleDegraded with a background re-probe'. So: probe runs pre-ready-write and a failure is loudly surfaced + background-re-probed, but `ready` is still written on probe failure. | 242 | gateway-4706 | [#4706](https://github.com/openova-io/openova/issues/4706) | 2-region BCP admission floor: a 1-region POST with no explicit bcpTopology is rejected at the LIVE production API. | mothership-2026-08-02 | ⛔ | mothership-2026-08-02T02:53Z WALK - BLOCKED at the surface the row names. The assertion is explicitly about the LIVE production API, so that endpoint was probed directly: GET https://console.openova.io/sovereign/api/v1/deployments -> 503, /sovereign/api/v1/healthz -> 503. No POST was attempted: with the API returning 503 a rejected POST would prove nothing about the admission floor - it would prove the backend is absent, which is already known. IMPORTANT DISTINCTION, verified not assumed: the EDGE is UP and routing. The response is a well-formed HTTP/2 503 with content-type/x-content-type-options/date headers, and /healthz on the same host returns 404 rather than 503 - different paths give different answers, so the gateway is serving and discriminating. What is missing is the catalyst-api backend (catalyst ns = 0 pods, #5558), NOT the gateway. Reading this as 'the edge is down' would send the next walker to the wrong layer. Promotion needs catalyst-api Running; then POST a 1-region body with no explicit bcpTopology and require a 4xx rejection. |
